The same form MUST be used in: UI contract authoring (`primary_tasks` slot per REQ-0115), iterate emit (`iter-NN/<screen-id>.{png,html,review.json}`), validator expectation, aggregate-dir filename (`.qfai/evidence/prototyping/screenshots/<screen-id>.png`, `.qfai/evidence/prototyping/html/<screen-id>.html`), and the `evidenceRefs[].path` field in `iterations[i]` (REQ-0111). Existing hyphen-form iter outputs are accepted during the deprecation window with `D-DEPRECATED-PATH` warning. - Chosen option: A (underscore). - Rejected option B — hyphen everywhere: requires validator + filename rewrites; conflicts with Python/YAML convention used in surrounding artifacts; the URL-friendliness argument is weaker than the file-stem ergonomics one. - DO NOT: normalize to hyphen. Temptation: URL routes use hyphen. - Rejected option C — accept both (validator change): silently masks drift; two equivalent IDs can refer to the same screen with no enforcement; defeats the SSOT premise of `screens[].id`. - DO NOT: accept both casings. Temptation: minimum-breakage path. - Source REQ: REQ-0116, REQ-0111. - Trace: discussion-20260523221141355 retired OQ (deferred → resolved here). #### DR-0001-0008: `validate.json` profile disambiguation — option A (profile-suffixed + always-latest pointer) (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-24 - Statement: `qfai validate --profile <p>` writes `.qfai/report/validate-<profile>.json` per profile AND `.qfai/report/validate.json` always reflects the most recent run with an explicit top-level `profile` field. `certify` (and all downstream skills) MUST read the profile-suffixed file when scoping by profile, and MAY read the non-suffixed file when the `profile` field check is sufficient. The doc-vs-impl drift `.qfai/output/validate.json` (old) vs `.qfai/report/validate.json` (current) resolves to `.qfai/report/validate.json`; the old path emits `D-DEPRECATED-PATH` warning during the window and is removed at the sunset version (qfai 1.10.0 per `package.json#version`). - Chosen option: A (profile-suffixed + always-latest pointer with explicit `profile` field). - Rejected option B — `certify` auto-reruns `qfai validate --profile prototyping` before reading: hides upstream drift (operator-driven validate-run state is silently overwritten by certify-driven re-run); adds latency; obscures which command actually produced the validated result. - DO NOT: have certify silently re-run validate. Temptation: "always fresh" feels safe. - Rejected option C — error out on profile mismatch and tell operator which command to re-run: worse UX than A (forces operator round-trip); does not prevent the silent overwrite if two profiles are run in sequence. - DO NOT: error on profile mismatch instead of disambiguating. Temptation: minimum-implementation path. - Source REQ: REQ-0120, REQ-0124. - Trace: discussion-20260523221141355 retired OQ (deferred → resolved here). #### DR-0001-0009: `primarySpecId` normalization — SHOULD remains SHOULD per REQ-0119 (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-24 - Statement: REQ-0119 SHOULD (input normalization of `primarySpecId` accepting `1` / `"1"` / `"01"` / `"0001"`) remains SHOULD. Implementations MAY accept any positive-integer-shaped input and MUST normalize internally to `spec-NNNN`; implementations that choose strict-rejection-only MUST produce the explicit error text from REQ-0119: `primarySpecId must be a 4-digit zero-padded string (e.g. "0001"); received <input>`. The error text contract is MUST regardless of which path the implementation chooses. No schema migration is forced. - Chosen option: SHOULD remains SHOULD (per REQ-0119 wording). - Rejected option — MUST-normalize: forces every implementation site (config loader, CLI flag parser, validator) to apply the normalization; schema migration for downstream consumers that already use `"0001"` form gains nothing; consumers that use `1` would need to be told they must change anyway because the spec-side artifacts still use 4-digit form. - DO NOT: promote the SHOULD to MUST in this pack. Temptation: "be lenient on input" feels user-friendly. - Source REQ: REQ-0119. - Trace: discussion-20260523221141355 retired OQ (deferred → resolved here). | DONE |
| DEC-0590 | .qfai/spec/_policies/08_Decisions.md#DR-0261: DR-0261..0274: Second-Wave Defect-Remediation pack — deferred OQ resolutions (2026-05-27) | Source: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ + the REQ-0157 scaffold-escalate residual + retired OQ (`/qfai-sdd` slice-time resolutions). These rows pin the specific implementation contract the per-spec slices (Spec A–K) cite. The acceptance signals for the underlying REQs (REQ-0150..0173) are invariant across each option set; the choices below select one option so downstream implementation, validation, and reviewer-gate code can be written against a single resolution. ID note: the next free plain `DR-NNNN` at authoring time was `DR-0261` (DR-0258/0259/0260 were consumed by CHG-003), so this family begins at DR-0261. retired OQ (memory schema), retired OQ (per-rule `proseCritique` configurability) and retired OQ (`/proposal` family) remain `deferred` (ops-owned) and are NOT decided here. #### DR-0261: Cycle-0 skeleton emission UX shape — token-driven placeholder (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: `qfai prototyping iterate --cycle 0 --emit-skeletons` emits one placeholder HTML per `screens[].id` in `frozenSurfaceUnion` by consuming the `DESIGN.md` tokens (color / font / radius / shadow) for default styling — option B (token-driven placeholder). No per-screen LLM generation call is made at cycle 0; subsequent cycles refine. The runtime-tunable `--skeleton-mode` flag (retired OQ register row → DR-0272) lets a project escalate to higher-fidelity modes later. Acceptance signal (every `frozenSurfaceUnion` screen has `evidenceRefs[]` of both kinds after convergence) is unchanged. - Chosen option: B (token-driven placeholder). - Rejected option A — full generation per declared screen: spends cycle-0 LLM budget on screens that the operator may never prioritize; highest cost for the union sizes typical of 7-spec projects; regresses the v1.9.1 cost posture. - DO NOT: make cycle-0 emit a full per-screen generation. Temptation: highest fidelity feels best. - Rejected option C — minimal HTML stub (`<main>`/`<header>`/`<footer>`, no token-driven content): produces shallow evidence that does not exercise the DESIGN.md token mirror; reviewers cannot judge brand compliance from an unstyled stub. - DO NOT: ship a token-blind stub. Temptation: cheapest to implement. - Source REQ: REQ-0150. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec A). #### DR-0262: DESIGN.md patch-zone shape — front-matter `patch_zone:` block (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: DESIGN.md gains a front-matter `patch_zone:` block (option a) listing the line ranges or token names that may be edited without invalidating `frozenDesignMdHash#majorHash`. Edits whose diff is fully contained in the patch zone update only a new `patchHash` field; `majorHash` (and therefore prototyping evidence validity) is unchanged. Edits touching any line/token outside the zone (or removing the `patch_zone:` block itself) invalidate evidence as today. No new lock-file artifact is introduced. Reviewer Gate emits `R-DESIGN-MD-PATCH-OUT-OF-ZONE` (severity warning) on out-of-zone edits. - Chosen option: a (front-matter `patch_zone:` block). - Rejected option b — generationally-versioned `DESIGN.md.lock.yaml` with `major.minor.patch`: introduces a second SSOT file and new lock-generation semantics (more ceremony) for the same invariant; the front-matter block keeps brand SSOT in one file (consistent with DCON-030 single-file posture). - DO NOT: add a generational lock-file for patch-zone semantics. Temptation: "versioned lock feels more disciplined". - Rejected option c — hybrid (front-matter + lock-file marker): combines both costs with no additional safety. - DO NOT: ship both surfaces. Temptation: cover every preference. - Source REQ: REQ-0151. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec B). #### DR-0263: exploration-mode gate-relaxation table — medium (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: Under `prototyping.mode: exploration`, downgrade `QFAI-CRIT-008` (convergence) AND the design-compliance error (DESIGN.md compliance / `designMdViolations`) from error → warning. Structural gates (schema / required-field / path) and license gates (exit 66) remain hard error. This is the medium relaxation between option A (only `QFAI-CRIT-008`) and option C (entire `QFAI-CRIT-*` family). `certify` rejects sealing any iteration produced under exploration mode regardless of the table (`R-EXPLORATION-CERTIFY-ATTEMPT`). - Chosen option: medium (`QFAI-CRIT-008` + design-compliance → warning; structural/license hard). - Rejected option A — only `QFAI-CRIT-008`: design-compliance error still blocks shallow exploration loops, which is the second-most-cited blocker; too narrow to make exploration usable. - DO NOT: relax only the axes gate. Temptation: safest minimal step. - Rejected option C — whole `QFAI-CRIT-*` family + advisory-failing findings: relaxes structural/license gates that protect evidence integrity; an exploration run could then produce structurally invalid artifacts that leak into later convergence. - DO NOT: relax structural or license gates in exploration mode. Temptation: maximum flexibility. - Source REQ: REQ-0152. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec C). #### DR-0264: Stale review-pack TTL default — 14d, project-configurable (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: `qfai doctor --clean` archives `.qfai/review/<ts>/` directories older than a TTL of 14 days by default, configurable via `qfai.config.yaml#review.staleTtlDays`. Archival moves to `.qfai/review/_archive/<ts>/` and never deletes; restore is a manual `mv` back. `qfai validate --profile review` treats `_archive/` as out-of-scope. 14d sits between the high-churn (SRC-0020, 4 packs in 4 days) and low-churn (SRC-0025, weeks) extremes; the config key lets either churn profile tune it. - Chosen option: 14d default + `review.staleTtlDays` config. - Rejected option — 7d fixed: too aggressive for low-churn projects that revisit packs across weeks; would archive packs still in active review. - DO NOT: hard-code a 7-day TTL. Temptation: keep the review dir small. - Rejected option — 30d fixed: high-churn projects accumulate a dozen stale packs before archival fires (the exact `QFAI-REVIEW-003/004/005` storm SRC-0020 reported). - DO NOT: hard-code a 30-day TTL. Temptation: safest long default with no churn signal. - Rejected option — config-only with no default: forces every project to set the key before archival works; breaks zero-config UX. - DO NOT: ship without a default. Temptation: avoid choosing a number. - Source REQ: REQ-0153. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec D). #### DR-0265: `QFAI-MOCK-010` direction — anchor-form template default + strict validator (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: Option b — the `qfai-discussion` template emits anchor-form (`<a href="#<name>">`) by default and SKILL.md instructs authors to use anchors in `03_Story-Workshop.md` HTML mocks. The `QFAI-MOCK-010` validator continues to PASS anchor URLs (`#name`) and external URLs (`http(s)://...`) and continues to FAIL same-origin absolute paths (`<a href="/products/">`) — it is NOT broadened. Template ↔ validator form a new SSOT-sync pair (Pair V) mirroring the v1.9.1 `R-PROMPT-SCANNER-DRIFT` precedent; asymmetric edits emit `R-MOCK-HREF-DRIFT`. - Chosen option: b (template anchor default; validator stays strict). - Rejected option a — broaden validator to accept same-origin absolute hrefs: relaxes a deterministic gate; mocks would then encode routes that the prototype reality cannot serve, re-introducing the "looks navigable but is not" mock drift. - DO NOT: accept `/<path>/` same-origin absolute hrefs in the validator. Temptation: most natural for web-URL-familiar authors. - Rejected option c — hybrid (validator accepts both + template emits anchor): the permissive validator silently masks template drift; the SSOT-sync pair loses teeth. - DO NOT: make the validator permissive while the template is strict. Temptation: reduce author friction maximally. - Source REQ: REQ-0154. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec E). #### DR-0266: Active discussion session pointer — `state.json#discussion.currentId` SSOT (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: Option B — `.qfai/state.json#discussion.currentId` is the single SSOT for the active discussion session. `qfai discussion list --active` is a READ view over it (not an independent source). All downstream skills read the pointer through one helper. Multiple-active ambiguity (no `currentId`, or `currentId` resolving to a missing/duplicate pack) is rejected with an error naming the candidate dirs and the recovery command (`qfai discussion use <id>`). - Chosen option: B (state.json SSOT) with A as a read view. - Rejected option A-alone — `qfai discussion list --active` derives from filesystem timestamps: timestamp inference is non-deterministic across multi-session / multi-user clones and gives no place to record an explicit operator choice. - DO NOT: infer the active pointer from mtime. Temptation: zero-config. - Rejected option C — `qfai.config.yaml#discussion.currentId`: config is committed/shared project policy, but the active session is per-runtime ephemeral state; `state.json` is already the runtime-state surface and is the correct home. - DO NOT: put ephemeral session state in committed config. Temptation: one config file for everything. - Source REQ: REQ-0155. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec E). #### DR-0267: `primary_tasks` recommended count band — 3..7 (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: The `QFAI-AUD-020` recommended `primary_tasks` count band is **3..7 tasks** per screen. Documented in the `ui-spec.yaml` template comments and `references/ui-contract-guide.md`; the warning text names the band. Below 3 risks under-specified screens; above 7 risks an unfocused screen. 3..7 is the permissive-but-bounded band (register option C) appropriate for the multi-screen SaaS surfaces the reporter projects build. - Chosen option: 3..7 (permissive). - Rejected option — 2..5: the lower floor of 2 admits screens that barely justify a UI contract; the reporter projects routinely declared 5–6 primary tasks on dashboard screens that 2..5 would flag as "too many". - DO NOT: cap the recommended ceiling at 5. Temptation: tighter discipline. - Rejected option — 1..3 (minimal): floor of 1 defeats the purpose of the band; too strict for real dashboards. - DO NOT: use a 1..3 band. Temptation: minimal surface. - Source REQ: REQ-0164. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec I). #### DR-0268: `primary_tasks` structured shape JSON Schema — `{id,label,acceptance}` all required (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: The structured `primary_tasks[]` item schema is `{ id: string, label: string, acceptance: string }` with all three required and `additionalProperties: false`. `auditProfile.ts` accepts BOTH this structured form AND the legacy string-only form during the deprecation window (`D-DEPRECATED-*` warn on string-only at sunset). The closed schema matches the existing US/TC `{id, label, acceptance}` convention so downstream coverage/atdd tooling can consume it without per-project shape negotiation. - Chosen option: `{id, label, acceptance}` all-required, closed. - Rejected option — minimal `{id, label}`: drops the `acceptance` field that makes a task testable; downstream atdd scaffolding (REQ-0157) would have nothing to anchor a TODO assertion to. - DO NOT: omit `acceptance`. Temptation: smallest schema. - Rejected option — full `{id, label, acceptance, priority?, owner?}` with `additionalProperties: true`: open shape invites per-project field sprawl that breaks cross-project tooling; `priority`/`owner` are speculative (YAGNI). - DO NOT: open the schema or add speculative fields. Temptation: future-proofing. - Source REQ: REQ-0164. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec I). #### DR-0269: SKILL.md Default Autopilot Policy template — 3 named buckets (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: Every SKILL.md `## Default Autopilot Policy` section uses three named buckets. (a) **auto-decide** (named defaults, AI decides without prompting): output formatting, ID / sequence numbering, append-vs-create when subject overlaps an existing artifact, and option-pick among demonstrably-equivalent alternatives. (b) **ask-user** (AI prompts via AskUserQuestion): approval-required triage ops (CREATE / DELETE / SPLIT / MERGE / SUPERSEDE / UPDATE:REMOVE), destructive operations, version-pin changes, and scope expansions. (c) **hard-required** (no default possible; must be supplied before proceeding): brand intent, `primarySpecId` when absent. The three enumerations are the prototype: a skill MAY narrow ANY of the three buckets (drop an entry it cannot reach) but MUST NOT widen one. Only auto-decide widening is machine-detectable (its entry set is closed) and surfaces `R-AUTOPILOT-POLICY-WIDENED` (warning); ask-user / hard-required are enforced by review. Reviewer Gate emits `R-AUTOPILOT-POLICY-MISSING` (error) when the section is absent. - Amendment (2026-08-22): the narrowing permission originally covered auto-decide only, so `/qfai-implement` shipped `/qfai-sdd`'s triage ops in ask-user and init's branding inputs in hard-required — decisions and inputs an implement run never reaches. Narrowing now applies to all three buckets; widening stays forbidden in all three. - Amendment 2 (2026-08-22): narrowing alone left `/qfai-implement` with no legal way to name the authorizations it DOES need. Its `TDDLIST-001` accepted-risk waiver and its Drift-Protocol Change-Request escalation are the same kind of decision as `/qfai-sdd`'s triage ops — a human authorization the skill cannot grant itself — but neither is a triage op, so listing them read as widening. **The ask-user bucket's first entry is therefore a category, not a fixed op list: _approval-required governance operations_, which each skill instantiates with the operations its own run requires human authorization for.** `/qfai-sdd` instantiates it as the triage ops (CREATE / DELETE / SPLIT / MERGE / SUPERSEDE / UPDATE:REMOVE); `/qfai-implement` instantiates it as the `TDDLIST-001` waiver approval and the Change-Request escalation. Instantiating a category with the skill's own operations is **not** widening; introducing a **new category** is, and stays forbidden. The other three ask-user entries (destructive operations, version-pin changes, scope expansions) were already category-shaped and are unchanged. - Amendment 4 (2026-09-12): narrowing the buckets to the operations a skill performs left the interview skills with no legal entry. `/qfai-grilling` and `/qfai-grill` perform the asking itself: a frontier decision and the confirmation that closes a session are what those runs do, and neither is a triage op, a destructive operation, a version pin or a scope expansion — so listing them read as widening. **The ask-user bucket gains a fifth category: _a decision a declared grilling session puts to the user_, available only to a skill whose own operation is the interview.** A skill that runs a session inside some other operation does not instantiate it: the session's questions are the session's, and `.agents/rules/grilling.md` owns which are asked. The category is closed the way the first one is — instantiating it with the skill's own questions is not widening, and a new category still is. - Amendment 3 (2026-09-07): the hard-required bucket is machine-checked as well. `QFAI-AUTOPILOT-001` reads every shipped `qfai-*` SKILL.md and reports a bucket naming an entry this decision has retired, or one no skill declares — a warning until the 1.12.0 release and an error from there. The statement above says only auto-decide widening is detectable because its entry set is closed; hard-required's is closed too, by the common entries plus a per-skill declaration, so the same reading applies. `companyName` is the retired entry: no shipped file read it, so the bucket paid a guaranteed prompt out of a 0-1 budget and read nothing back. ask-user remains enforced by review — its first entry is a category each skill instantiates, which no closed set describes. - Chosen option: per-category opinionated template (register option C) with the three buckets above as the prototype. - Rejected option A — conservative (most decisions ask user): preserves the 3–6 avoidable `AskUserQuestion` per session that this pack exists to remove. - DO NOT: default to asking on procedural decisions. Temptation: maximize governance. - Rejected option B — aggressive (most decisions auto-decide): erodes governance by auto-deciding triage / destructive / scope / version operations that require human authorization. - DO NOT: auto-decide approval-required or destructive operations. Temptation: maximize autopilot. - Source REQ: REQ-0160. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec H). #### DR-0270: Envelope-deviation `AskUserQuestion` audit trigger taxonomy (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: The audit-log write (REQ-0158) triggers when an `AskUserQuestion` template names any of the four envelope-deviation contexts: "skill envelope", "architectural decision", "rejected-option re-adoption", or "scope expansion". On trigger, the skill body writes `.qfai/evidence/decisions/<ISO8601-ts>.json` `{question, answer, scope, operatorIdentity, timestamp, envelopeContractClause}`. (This is the register's option C — a per-skill declared list of envelope contexts — pinned to a fixed four-context taxonomy so the trigger is deterministic and reviewable rather than a heuristic regex.) `.qfai/evidence/decisions/` is tracked in version control (governance record; negated in the managed `.gitignore` block). - Chosen option: fixed four-context declared taxonomy (register option C, pinned). - Rejected option A — explicit `AskUserQuestion({envelopeDeviation: true})` parameter: requires every skill author to remember to set the flag; a missed flag silently drops the audit record (fail-open). - DO NOT: depend on a per-call boolean flag. Temptation: most explicit. - Rejected option B — question-text regex heuristic: brittle; false-positives on routine prompts, false-negatives on reworded ones. - DO NOT: use a free-text regex trigger. Temptation: zero author burden. - Source REQ: REQ-0158. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec G). #### DR-0271: `qfai audit log` CLI shape (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: `qfai audit log` (SHOULD-level, REQ-0171) lists `.qfai/evidence/decisions/<ts>.json` records newest-first with filters `--scope`, `--operator`, `--clause` (filters on `envelopeContractClause`) and `--format table\|json` defaulting to `table`. This is register option B (filtered query) with a default human-readable table view; it ships the filter surface up front because ops audit is the primary use case named in REQ-0171. - Chosen option: B (filtered query) + `--format table\|json`, table default. - Rejected option A — simple newest-first JSON list, no filters: ships fastest but forces operators to pipe through external `jq` for the scope/operator/clause queries REQ-0171 explicitly calls out. - DO NOT: ship list-only with no filters. Temptation: fastest to build. - Rejected option C — tree / decision-graph rendering: over-engineered for an advisory ergonomic surface (YAGNI); the records are flat JSON. - DO NOT: build graph rendering. Temptation: richest UX. - Source REQ: REQ-0171. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec G). #### DR-0272: `D-SCAFFOLD-PLACEHOLDER` escalate cycle count — error after 3 validate cycles (REQ-0157 escalate residual resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: `qfai validate` emits `D-SCAFFOLD-PLACEHOLDER` (severity warning) for an atdd-scaffold skeleton whose `// TODO: implement assertion for <TC-ID>` is still present; the warning escalates to **error after 3 validate cycles** with the placeholder unremoved, configurable via `qfai.config.yaml#atdd.scaffoldEscalateCycles`. Three cycles gives an operator a normal red→green TDD turnaround before the placeholder hard-blocks completion-claim, while preventing indefinite placeholder accumulation. (This resolves the escalate-count that REQ-0157 left "default deferred to /qfai-sdd"; the `--skeleton-mode` runtime-tunability of register retired OQ is recorded separately at DR-0273.) - Chosen option: error after 3 cycles, configurable. - Rejected option — never escalate (warning-only): placeholders accumulate silently; an operator can claim TC coverage with empty assertions indefinitely. - DO NOT: leave the placeholder a permanent warning. Temptation: avoid blocking. - Rejected option — escalate after 1 cycle: too aggressive; blocks the very next validate before the operator has had a TDD cycle to fill the assertion. - DO NOT: escalate on the first cycle. Temptation: strictest signal. - Source REQ: REQ-0157. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 (REQ-0157 escalate residual; Spec F). #### DR-0273: Cycle-0 skeleton mode runtime control — `--skeleton-mode` flag (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: The skeleton emission mode is exposed as a CLI flag `qfai prototyping iterate --skeleton-mode full\|placeholder\|stub` (register option A), defaulting to `placeholder` per DR-0261. Config-key form is not added. The flag lets a project that wants higher-fidelity cycle-0 evidence escalate per-run without a config edit, while the default keeps the DR-0261 cost posture. - Chosen option: A (CLI flag, default `placeholder`). - Rejected option B — config-driven `qfai.config.yaml#prototyping.skeletonMode`: a per-run cost trade-off is better expressed as a per-invocation flag than committed project policy. - DO NOT: bury skeleton-mode in config. Temptation: one config for all knobs. - Rejected option C — hardcoded per DR-0261 with no override: removes operator control that some union sizes legitimately need. - DO NOT: hardcode with no escape hatch. Temptation: fewest surfaces. - Source REQ: REQ-0150. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 retired OQ (Spec A). #### DR-0274: Pack-location lint scope — staged/changed dirs vs allowed roots (retired OQ resolved) - Date: 2026-05-27 - Statement: `packages/qfai/scripts/check-pack-locations.mjs` scans staged / changed directories for `review-*/` or `discussion-*/` directories that fall outside the allowed roots (`tmp/`, `.qfai/review/<ts>/`, `.qfai/discussion/<ts>/`) and emits `R-PACK-LOCATION-DRIFT` referencing `.agents/rules/root-additions-policy.md` and the corrected path. Wired into `pnpm ci:lint`. Scanning staged/changed dirs (not a full-tree walk) keeps the lane fast and scoped to the PR's actual additions. (This resolves retired OQ's lint-scope dimension for REQ-0167; the register's retired OQ `sdd lint --fix` autofix question for `surface_type` remains separately deferred — see note below.) - Chosen option: staged/changed-dir scope against the three allowed roots. - Rejected option — full-tree walk every run: re-flags pre-existing legacy packs on unrelated PRs; noisy and slow. - DO NOT: full-walk on every lint. Temptation: catch everything. - Source REQ: REQ-0167. Trace: discussion-20260527075558258 (REQ-0167 lint scope; Spec K). NOTE: the register row retired OQ (`qfai sdd lint --fix` autofix for `surface_type`-absent specs, Spec I) is a distinct question and remains `deferred` per `13_Deferred.md`. | DONE |
| DEC-0591 | .qfai/spec/_policies/08_Decisions.md#DR-0275: DR-0275..0276: CHG-007 — Layered CI Test Scaffold Adoption (2026-08-05) | Source: discussion-20260804173914356 (REQ-0001..0025, NFR-0001..0016, DTC-1..28), resolving that pack's deferred `retired OQ` (spec and capability allocation). ApprovedBy: user@2026-08-05. These two rows are the policy-layer allocation decisions the per-spec slices cite; the full change record, its Triage Table and its impact-cascade verification live in `_policies/10_delta.md` § `2026-08-05 — CHG-007`, and the two MUST stay consistent. New plain `DR-NNNN` IDs begin at DR-0275. **Six** new open questions are recorded in `_policies/09_Open-questions.md` and are NOT decided here: `retired OQ` (make the CAP-to-spec gate number-based, solution-architect), `retired OQ` (`spec-0005`'s nominal traceability chain, qa-strategist), `retired OQ` (spec-claimed implementation paths that do not exist, solution-architect) and `retired OQ` (the permanent drift blind spot for adopters who installed a shipped workflow before the provenance record existed — owner: **user**, the one user-owned question this change surfaced) and `retired OQ` (duplicate item-ID definitions inside one spec are undetected by every validator — measured on a spec this change never touched, qa-strategist) and `retired OQ`. The sixth is `retired OQ`: no validator reconciles a delta's declared ID ranges against the pack's actual contents (qa-strategist). #### DR-0275: revoke the `spec-0017` / `CAP-0017` permanent-gap reservation - Date: 2026-08-05 - Decision: the 2026-05-06 permanent-gap reservation on `spec-0017` and `CAP-0017` is revoked. Both return to being ordinary reusable IDs, and `spec-0017` is renumbered immediately as `CAP-0017 = Repository Toolchain`. - Context: `validateSpecSplitByCapability` derives its expected spec set **positionally** from the CAP count — the expected list is built by mapping each CAP's list **index** to `spec-000<index+1>` (`packages/qfai/src/core/validators/specSplitByCapability.ts:75`), so actual CAP numbers are never read. With 17 CAP rows it expects `spec-0001..spec-0017`. A 17th capability therefore had **no legal name**: `spec-0017` violated the reservation, and `spec-0018` raised `QFAI-SPLIT-103` (missing `spec-0017`) **and** `QFAI-SPLIT-104` (extra `spec-0018`) simultaneously, both at `error`. - Rationale: a reserved gap ID — at **any** position — and a positional 1:1 gate are mutually unsatisfiable from the moment the CAP count reaches that ordinal. Because the expected set only ever grows, a reservation is never permanently safe; it is only not-yet-due. The reservation was therefore not merely inconvenient but structurally unmaintainable — it blocked every future capability addition, not just this one. Renumbering is the only state that satisfies both the gate and the catalog. - Rejected: keep the reservation and name the new spec `spec-0018` - DO NOT: leave **any** gap ID reserved while the 1:1 gate is positional — a trailing reservation is not safer than an inner one, only later. Temptation: honouring a documented reservation feels like the conservative choice, but here it produces a permanently red validate. - Rejected: keep the reservation and make the validator ID-based (pair `CAP-NNNN` with `spec-NNNN` by number) in this same change - DO NOT: bundle a validator behaviour change into a spec-allocation change. Temptation: "fix the root cause once and for all" — but the validator is CAP-0004 surface with its own tests, and `08_Decisions.md` DR-0001-0005 forbids inflating a pack beyond an atomic slice. Recorded as `retired OQ` instead. - Residual risk: §ID Stability Rule 5's default (leave a gap) still contradicts the positional gate for **any** future DELETE / SUPERSEDE, wherever the gap falls, once the capability count reaches that ordinal — inner-range or trailing is irrelevant, as CHG-007 itself demonstrated (`spec-0017` was a **trailing** gap and detonated at the very next capability addition). §ID Stability Rule 5 now states this position-independently as a **rule**, not as an inner-gap exception; see `11_Slice-Policy.md` §ID Stability Rule 5. `retired OQ` tracks making the validator ID-based so the default stops being a trap. - Evidence: `packages/qfai/src/core/validators/specSplitByCapability.ts:60-131`; `_policies/11_Slice-Policy.md` §ID Stability Rule 5 + §Current Slicing Notes. - Related: `CAP-0017`, `spec-0017`, `_policies/07_Constraints.md` OC-71, `_policies/11_Slice-Policy.md`. Source REQ: REQ-0001..0025 (precondition of the allocation). Trace: discussion-20260804173914356 retired OQ. #### DR-0276: `toolchain` as a fifth slice category - Date: 2026-08-05 - Decision: add a fifth slice category `toolchain` holding the repository's own tooling — `.github/workflows/`, the repository-internal composite actions under `.github/actions/**`, root `scripts/`, `packages/qfai/scripts/`, and the test-runner configuration (`vitest.config.ts` / `vitest.workspace.ts`) — as one collective spec. `.github/actions/**` is named explicitly because `scripts/verify-pack.mjs` bans composite actions only from the **shipped** `.github` tree (`assets/init/root/.github`); repository-internal ones are legal and would otherwise be the one toolchain surface with no owner. - Context: measured against the tree, **no spec owns** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `permissions:`, action pinning, or `vitest.workspace.ts`; all 11 root `scripts/*.mjs` are unclaimed by any pack, as is `packages/qfai/scripts/check-branch-version-pin.sh`. The four existing categories are each defined by a **distributed** product surface, so repository-internal tooling had no category. - Rationale: the gap is a category gap, not a CI gap. Filing only the CI work would leave the other orphans unowned and reproduce the same hole at the next toolchain change. - Boundary: shipped workflow templates (`assets/init/root/.github/workflows/**`) stay with **CAP-0003** (`qfai init`). Distributed-or-not is the boundary, so the two surfaces the discussion pack separates stay separated in the spec layer too. - Rejected: append the own-CI requirements to `spec-0004` - Reason: `spec-0004`'s subject is the `qfai validate` command; its implementation surface is `src/core/validators/**` + `src/cli/commands/validate.ts`, disjoint from `.github/workflows/**`. `10_delta.md:212` (do not cram different features into one spec) applies directly. - DO NOT: file GitHub Actions job topology under the validate command spec. Temptation: `spec-0004` already owns two `pnpm ci:lint` lanes, so it looks like the established home for "CI things". - Rejected: split the own-CI work across `spec-0004` (lint lane) / `spec-0009` (test config) / `spec-0011` (parallelism) - Reason: `spec-0009` scopes **adopter** repository config discovery, not QFAI's own workspace; `spec-0011`'s parallelism is agent/worktree-level, not CI-worker-level. All three would be scope escapes, and one design would be fragmented across three packs. - DO NOT: distribute one cohesive design across three specs. Temptation: every fragment individually looks like an append to something that already exists. - Consequences: `toolchain` is a collective category like `agent` — toolchain constituents do not each get their own spec. The category is repository-internal by construction, so nothing `spec-0017` owns is distributed; conversely every shipped-template requirement stays outside it and inside `CAP-0003`. - Related: `CAP-0017`, `spec-0017`, `spec-0003`, `_policies/11_Slice-Policy.md` §Slice Categories. Source REQ: REQ-0001..0013, REQ-0023, REQ-0025 (own-CI / toolchain rows); REQ-0014..0022, REQ-0024 stay on the distributed side. Trace: discussion-20260804173914356 retired OQ. | DONE |
| DEC-0592 | .qfai/spec/_policies/08_Decisions.md#DR-0277: `proseCritique` is a cap, and the unit is selected by the text | - Status: accepted - Date: 2026-09-11 - Context: `DR-0001-0003` mandated the band `200..500 words OR 600..2500 characters`. Two things in that statement are wrong about the rule QFAI-PROT-002 applies. The lower bound was removed: it failed a review for being short, which is a length a reviewer pads to reach, and it failed a review on the length of its own script rather than on what the script said. The OR was never how the unit is chosen, and choosing that way cannot work — an English critique carries no CJK characters, so it is under any character cap by construction, and a rule that passed on either unit would pass every English text however long. - Decision: `validateProseCritiqueBand` applies a **cap**, and **selects** the unit from the text. Text carrying CJK is measured in CJK characters against `PROSE_CRITIQUE_MAX_CJK_CHARS`; anything else is measured in whitespace- separated words against `PROSE_CRITIQUE_MAX_WORDS`. Only the selected unit's cap applies, and there is no lower bound in either unit. The error names the unit measured, the cap and the count. - Consequences: a critique of any length up to its cap passes, so a spec, acceptance criterion or test case stating a floor states something the code does not do. It also forecloses the English word semantic `DR-0001-0003` protected: a short English critique is no longer distinguishable from a short filler one by length, and nothing measures that. A script whose writing system separates no words and is not CJK — Thai is the clearest case — counts as very few words, so the word cap does not bind on it. That is a cap that does not apply rather than a text rejected wrongly, and binding it needs per-script segmentation. - Related: supersedes `DR-0001-0003`. `spec-0012` REQ-0012-0059, `spec-0004` REQ-0028, QFAI-PROT-002. | DONE |

| DEC-0729 | Step 9 restores an absent roster path only on a hold that shows a repair emptied it | Approved by the user on 2026-09-25, from the architecture review of step 9. A repoint moves the old link into a hold, `<link>.qfai-repair-<n>/`, before it writes the new one. A hold now means one thing: a qfai repair emptied this path and nothing has written it since. Every writer that creates a link at a path removes the holds beside it that hold a link to the old or the new target: `qfai init`, `qfai doctor` and the migration alike. A hold matches by the same name pattern prune and the gate use, not by prefix. Step 9 reads each roster path: an absent path with such a hold is restored and its hold removed; an absent path without one stays absent, even where the gate reports it missing, because the project may have removed it; a plural link is repointed; a link already naming the singular directory with a hold beside it has only the hold removed; a link naming neither directory is left as it is and listed under `## For a person` when the gate names it. A hold is removed by unlinking the link it holds and then a non-recursive `rmdir`, so a hold that gained other content keeps it and the run reports the removal as incomplete. The removal is part of the repoint: each removed hold is a target of the `repoint host integration link` operation, so the dry run lists it and the real run removes only listed holds (NFR-0002). This keeps BR-0507: restoring on a hold undoes the repair's own emptying and never recreates a wrapper the project removed, and a hold is qfai's own record rather than project content, so step 9 still repoints managed links and changes nothing else. When the platform refuses a symlink (Windows without Developer Mode), the held link goes back by `rename` while the path is empty, so a refused repoint leaves the old link in place and no hold. Related: BR-0507, AC-0004-0011-01, EX-0004-0011-01, NFR-0001, NFR-0002, `.qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/qfai-migration-spec-to-story.md`. | DONE |
| DEC-0730 | Step 9 rewrites a respelled working link, and init and doctor clear a stale hold beside a current link | Approved by the user on 2026-09-25, from the final architecture review of step 9; it extends DEC-0729. The link gate and `qfai init` judge a managed link by one rule: the target as written, normalised for the platform, must equal the target init writes. (a) A link that reaches the singular directory under another spelling, such as an absolute path or, on Windows, another letter case, is one the gate reports, so step 9 rewrites it to the gate's form the way it repoints a plural link: the old link moves into a hold first and goes back if the platform refuses the new one, and the dry run lists the rewrite. (b) A hold beside a link that is already current is left by a repair that stopped after writing the new link; `qfai init` and `qfai doctor` remove it as well, listing it in a dry run, and the migration removes only the holds its plan lists. Both stay within BR-0507 and NFR-0002. Related: DEC-0729, BR-0507, NFR-0002, `.qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/qfai-migration-spec-to-story.md`. | DONE |

A story's `01_User-story.md` and `02_Acceptance-Criteria.md` and every contract statement stay out of `recordAreas`. The owner of a `test_fix` is the kind of the diagnosis's first matched ID: a BF or an AC goes to `qfai-atdd`, an EX to `qfai-implement`, as `rule/test-layers.md` maps them. - Realized by: BR-0578, BR-0655. | SUPERSEDED (by DEC-2048) |

The tracked summary binds `{ slotId, flowId, storyIds }` and a debt names its `owningFlow`. - Realized by: BR-0540; `qfai-workflow.md#route-proposal`, `workflow-files.md#tracked-tree`. | SUPERSEDED (by DEC-2048) |

| DEC-0781 | A stage result names the agent that produced it | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator on the implementation-reviewer's finding on the workflow control-core slice. - Why: BR-0553 and BR-0580 judge a reviewer by the run's history of authors and recommenders, but no payload named who authored a result, so the history stayed empty: `reviewer-not-independent` never fired and `finish` accepted an author's own `qa-gatekeeper` PASS. - Decision: every stage result, a routing result included, carries a required `actor: { agentInstance }`; the core records it as the recommender of a routing result and the author of any other, and each reviewer of an accepted result as a reviewer, and refuses a review by the result's own actor (BR-0747, EX-0001-0187-08). | DEC-0786 | Change request: .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/qfai-prototyping.md, .qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/qfai-prototyping-iterate.md, .qfai/spec/02_business-flow/business-flow-0001/user-story-retired/03_Example.md | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Approved by the user on 2026-09-26 through the structured question tool. - Why: BR-0267 says `design-system.yaml` is generated post-loop as a deterministic mirror of the root `DESIGN.md` token tables, but no command wrote it; the `/qfai-prototyping` skill had the orchestrating agent write it by hand, which is neither deterministic nor checked before `qfai validate` reads it. - Options: 1. `qfai prototyping iterate` writes the file on the cycle that ends the loop, the convergence stop (exit 64) or the budget stop (exit 65), from the root `DESIGN.md` that invocation read (recommended, adopted). 2. A new public sub-command, `qfai prototyping mirror`, run by the skill after the loop (not selected: a fourth public sub-command for a step that has exactly one moment to run). 3. Keep the hand-written step (not selected: the defect this row answers). - Decision: option 1. An unreadable or malformed `DESIGN.md`, and a lock that is malformed, unreadable or differs from `DESIGN.md`, already exit 2 before the stop is evaluated, so nothing is written. A missing lock is allowed by iterate as before; the mirror records the hash of the `DESIGN.md` it copies, which equals the lock's whenever a lock exists. The skill's hand-written step is removed. - Changes: the iterate section and BR-0267 of `qfai-prototyping.md`, the convergence section of `qfai-prototyping-iterate.md`, and retired EX, whose "Post-handoff" timing becomes the cycle that ends the loop. | DONE |

| DEC-0788 | The cycle's finder is a test-design-analyst that wrote none of the BRs, and it raises five kinds of finding | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-26 in a confirmed discussion session. - Decision: the finder is an agent other than the BR author, a test-design-analyst. | DEC-0795 | The cycle's remaining rules were settled by a delegated grilling session | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Adopted by the orchestrator from the recommendation of the requirements-reviewer acting as griller, after two rounds with the requirements-analyst and the solution-architect. - Why: the user's decisions DEC-0787 to DEC-0794 left these points open, and the cycle cannot be specified without them. - Decision: (1) the finder reads the BRs whose Statement or Examples cell the invocation wrote or changed, the EXs they cite and the EXs the invocation wrote or changed, and no cycle runs when no such BR exists (BR-0748). (6) One griller per cycle adjudicates, and it is neither the finder nor an author of a targeted item (BR-0749). | DEC-0892 | Rejected finding G1a-C1-F32, granularity on AC-0001-0185-05, EX-0001-0185-11, -15, -16, -17, -18: splitting AC-0001-0185-05 so that the `start` inputs, the CLI operation set and one JSON document per call leave the "no stage typed" scenario | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Source: the whole-flow concrete-abstract cycle of BF-0001 the user asked for on 2026-09-26, run over every existing BR whose Examples cell cites an EX of the flow. - Rejected by the group's griller (requirements-reviewer). - Why: The EXs are the mechanics by which `qfai-run` "fetches and submits every work order itself", which is this AC's Then, so no rule mismatch shows the split. The split changes an existing story for grouping alone, and a new AC needs its own integration-layer annotating test under `acceptance_obligations_unmet`, so the finder's "none needed" understates the cost. | REJECTED |
| DEC-0893 | Rejected finding G1b-C1-F26, redundant-ex on BR-0747, EX-0001-0187-08, EX-0001-0187-03: merging EX-0001-0187-08 (an author recorded from each result's `actor`) into EX-0001-0187-03 (a reviewer found in a supplied actor history) | - Date: 2026-09-26 - Source: the whole-flow concrete-abstract cycle of BF-0001 the user asked for on 2026-09-26, run over every existing BR whose Examples cell cites an EX of the flow. - Rejected by the group's griller (requirements-reviewer). - Why: Not the same partition. Recording from `actor` and then enforcing is BR-0747's own subject, and only EX-0001-0187-08 shows it. Citing EX-0001-0187-03 from BR-0747 would duplicate BR-0580's partition. | REJECTED |

Besides making the managed block the installed package's, it removes every negation that re-includes `.qfai/evidence` or a path under it, inside or outside the block; deletes a legacy `.qfai/evidence/.gitignore`; removes every entry under `.qfai/evidence/` from the git index, as `git rm -r --cached .qfai/evidence` would, leaving the files on disk, running git from an argument vector and committing nothing; says why when the project is not a git repository or nothing is tracked there; and reports the count and each removed line. A dry run changes neither files nor the index, and a rerun changes nothing. `!.qfai/`, `!.qfai/install-provenance.json` and every `!.qfai/assistant` line stay. (2) The plan, the ID map and the `legacy/` and `retired/` archives stay under `.qfai/evidence/migration-spec-to-story/` and become local like the rest of evidence; the skill tells the person that the archived copies of customised files exist only in the working copy, and to keep a copy elsewhere if needed. (3) Step 12's `gitignore` check requires `.qfai/run/` and no negation re-including `.qfai/evidence/`, never flagging `!.qfai/`, and a path under `.qfai/evidence/` that git still tracks is an item for a person. (4) Every migration rule and BF-0004 story saying evidence is tracked is aligned. - Adopted while writing: (a) a `.qfai/evidence/.gitignore` that is a regular file is always deleted, because every line such a file can hold governs only paths under `.qfai/evidence/`, which the managed block now ignores whole; only that path held by something other than a regular file is left for a person. (b) Step 10 reports the git index under a new section, `## Git index`, rather than `## Operations`, because removing an index entry changes no file and `## Operations` must match the files the real run changes; BR-0510 and EX-0004-0012-02 therefore except the line step 10 prints there. (c) The tracked-path check is a step-12 check of its own, `evidence-tracked`, whose one item names every tracked path; a project that is not a git repository passes it. (d) The skill tells the person where `SKILL.md` has the AI report the migration done. - Supersedes, in DEC-0917, only the wording that has the managed `.gitignore` block carry `!.qfai/evidence/workflow/`: its step 11 item and the two `.gitignore` lines of its step 12 item. The rest of DEC-0917 stands, so its row keeps Status DONE. - Changing a criterion, an example or a rule in place under this row follows the story tree rule that an item existing when a change starts changes only under an in-force `Change request:` row; only the rows of `decisions.md` and `open-questions.md` are append-only. - Out of scope: the managed block itself, which `qfai-init.md#the-managed-gitignore-block` states. - New: AC-0004-0011-03 to -06, EX-0004-0011-03 to -09, AC-0004-0012-04, EX-0004-0012-05, EX-0004-0013-15 to -17; BR-0767 to BR-0773. Changed text: BR-0464, BR-0508, BR-0510, BR-0757, BR-0759, BR-0765; AC-0004-0011-02, EX-0004-0011-02, EX-0004-0012-02, AC-0004-0013-01, EX-0004-0013-01; the goal and non-goals of US-0004-0011. | DONE |

The story-tree document schemas declare no `## Decision Guardrails` section, so a conforming tree gives the command nothing to read. - Decided by the user: retire the command. `qfai guardrails` with its `list`, `extract` and `check` actions, its exports from the package entry point, the `guardrails.present` doctor check, and the report’s Decision Guardrails section and `guardrails` JSON field are removed. | DEC-0936 | Split the stage skills into steps and route by step | - Date: 2026-09-27 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-27. - Decision: the six large stage skills `qfai-discussion`, `qfai-sdd`, `qfai-prototyping`, `qfai-verify`, `qfai-atdd` and `qfai-implement` are split into small steps, and `qfai-maintain` becomes a skill with one step, `maintain-edit`. `qfai-run`, `qfai-grill`, `qfai-grilling`, `qfai-configure`, `qfai-migration-v1-to-v2` and `web-research` are not split. - Placement: each step is `.qfai/assistant/step/<name>/STEP.md`, one level deep, named `<owner>-<name>` with no `qfai-` prefix, and is never linked into a host skill directory. A step shared by several skills has owner `common` and a `common-` name. `requires` names only common steps, and a common step requires nothing. References and templates stay under their skill. - Skills: the thirteen host-visible skills keep their names. A skill that owns steps lists them in frontmatter `steps:` and runs them one at a time, reading only the current `STEP.md`, then runs one review with the union of the reviewers of the steps it ran. Its description states only when to use it, and its `roles:` is `orchestrator` plus the union of its steps' `roles:`. The entry check and the step loop every parent follows are stated once in `.qfai/assistant/rule/shared-skill-operating-baseline.md`, and the preamble shared by every skill moves there too; each `SKILL.md` keeps its own `## Default Autopilot Policy` buckets (BR-0351). - Runs: a plan stage names an ordered list of steps, and a step may carry its own predicate. The new predicate `proposed` runs a step only when the route proposal lists it in `optionalSteps`; a proposal naming a step the plan does not gate with `proposed` is refused `stage-set`. A work order carries its steps and the union of their reviewers, and a stage is reviewed once, after its last step. The Operations tables and each skill's `references/orchestrated-mode.md` are retired. The five routes and their predicates stay, and `finish` still requires this run's `verify.json` PASS and an independent `qa-gatekeeper` PASS. - Routing: routing entries and `qfai.config.yaml` overrides are keyed by step. A skill that owns steps has no review profile of its own. - The product is unreleased, so no alias, compatibility shim or migration of the old names is added. - Rejected: a `SKILL.md` for each step nested under its skill, because Codex registers nested skills while Claude Code and VS Code do not, so the hosts would disagree on what can be invoked; hidden top-level skills, because each host hides a skill with a different flag; a review at the end of every step, because it multiplies the reviews of a stage. - Amends: DEC-0765, whose sentence that each stage skill declares its operations in an Operations table no longer holds; plans stay built in and are still not an extension point. DEC-0009, as BR-0035 states it, gains the `step/` layer beside `rule/`, `skill/`, `agent/` and `prompt/`. Both rows are left as written. | DONE |

| DEC-0987 | Route requests through a fixed catalog chosen by decision rules, with three upward-only modifiers | - Date: 2026-09-28 - Decided by the user on 2026-09-28, who approved the whole design for implementation. - Evidence: 10,005 requests coded with one codebook — 1,155 from this repository's tracker, 7,650 issues from open-source projects and 1,200 discussions — into 30 intents and 44 work patterns. The ten most frequent patterns cover 61.1% of all items and 85.5% of this repository's; only all 44 cover the open-source tail. On completed items, intent plus entry flags fix the pattern 92.6% of the time once the exits decided by the outcome are set aside, and 35.0% of completed defect reports closed without a change after diagnosis. A second coder recoded 300 items: pattern agreement 76.7% (κ 0.76), 86.1% (κ 0.85) on high-confidence items, intent family 88.0% (κ 0.83), gate 91.3% but κ 0.41; route agreement with the merged catalog 79.7% (κ 0.78), and 72.0% (κ 0.65) on this repository's items. - Decision: (1) Thirty-nine fixed routes in seven families, each running every step of its plan. Adding or dropping steps per request is abolished: the `proposed` predicate, `optionalSteps`, every plan `when`, and the proposal's `candidateRoute` and `requiredStages` go. (2) Three modifiers, `review:heavy`, `gate:user` and `gate:release`: a closed set that only raises review and stops, never changes a step, attaches when any of its signals holds and is never lowered. (3) A closed list of pass-through steps that always run and pass with evidence when they have nothing to write; a pass whose obligation remains is refused. `sdd-story` also passes when a change stays inside the documents that own the truth and adds or changes no example. (4) The agent extracts intent, entry flags, qualifiers, signals, risks, gate, artifacts and confidence; the CLI decides the route with 29 ordered decision rules. (5) Doubt adds a gate: a low-confidence extraction adds `gate:user` and `review:heavy` and asks among the candidate routes; medium confidence adds nothing. (6) Exits without a change are routes, and their nine steps belong to a new parent skill, `qfai-triage`; `qfai-implement` gains 13 steps and `qfai-verify` 6. (7) Deciding and building stay separate routes; a run moves between routes only at declared branch points, at most twice without the operator, and keeps its evidence when the revision is unchanged. (8) `verify-change-note` opens the verify block of every change route. (9) The catalog has no size ceiling; every route added, removed or changed needs a person's approval, recorded as a `Change request:` row and checked in CI. (10) Route ids are verb-object names, and none of the five earlier ids is kept: `direct` becomes `edit-text`, `bugfix` `fix-defect`, `bounded-change` `add-feature`, `feature` `add-feature` or `prototype-feature`, and `discovery` `decide-design`. - Rejected: about 55 fixed routes with each gate and risk variant duplicated as a route, because gates and risks vary independently of the work sequence — one pattern needed the user's decision in only 41% of its items — so every variant doubles routes and thins their evaluation seeds. Composing a plan per request, because the work sequence is fixed at entry 92.6% of the time, no expected plan could be tested, and it restores the per-request step choice this decision abolishes. Seven consistency routes instead of four, because merging them raised route agreement on this repository's items from 68.0% to 72.0% with identical step sequences, and a separate route for aligning to a named owner lowered it to 69.0% (78.3% overall). A ceiling of 50 routes with a second wave held back, because the approval row already bounds growth. The agent choosing a route directly, because an ordered table can be tested exhaustively and its errors traced. One route that decides and then builds, because more than half of the requests to accept an idea never reach building and the two need different interviews. Gating on the extracted gate alone, because its κ is 0.41. - Where: the route catalog, decision rules, modifiers, pass-through checks and branch points in `cli-0015-qfai-workflow.md`; the plan format and the reading of retired route ids in `cli-0020-workflow-files.md`; the new steps in `cli-0002-assistant-steps.md`; `qfai-triage` and the `heavy` review profile in `cli-0001-assistant-routing.md`. The work lands in eleven pull requests: this specification, the engine, the step contents, the catalog, the decision rules, re-routing, extraction, the evaluation seeds and the documentation. | DONE |

.qfai/spec/03_contract/cli/cli-0020-workflow-files.md, .qfai/spec/decisions.md | - Date: 2026-09-28 - Requested by: the routing design DEC-0987 records. - Change: the workflow contract holds the route catalog, and this row approves each of its 39 routes: close-no-change, answer-question, investigate-question, request-info, close-duplicate, cluster-reports, decide-acceptance, decide-design, decompose-epic, retriage-bundle, repair-consistency, sweep-guard, retire-mechanism, restate-records, add-feature, prototype-feature, change-compatibility, apply-settled-spec, apply-settled-build, refactor-code, edit-text, fix-defect, fix-regression, improve-performance, fix-vulnerability, fix-crash, fix-intermittent, fix-env-bound, fix-conformance, quarantine-flaky, repair-test, fix-red-main, change-tooling, bump-dependency, revert-culprit, hand-off-operation, backport-fix, draft-release-notes and verify-manually. It also states the extraction, the 29 decision rules, the candidate question, the three modifiers, the pass-through steps and their checks, the branch points, the re-route cap, the three ways a route ends and the closure record (BR-0015-0185 to BR-0015-0282). The workflow files contract states the plan format — decision, release and branch points, default modifiers and step modes, and no `when` — the stage kinds with `sdd_delta` merged into `sdd`, the reading of retired route ids and the migration notes (BR-0020-0022 to BR-0020-0024 added). The assistant-steps contract adds the 28 steps with their owners and states what the routes ask of existing steps (BR-0002-0009 to BR-0002-0013); the assistant-routing contract adds `qfai-triage` and the `heavy` review profile (BR-0001-0034, BR-0001-0035). A diagnosis verdict gains seven values (BR-0004-0046). New stories US-0001-0211 to US-0001-0221 with their criteria and examples. - Revised to the new rules, keeping their IDs: BR-0015-0012, BR-0015-0017, BR-0015-0018, BR-0015-0024, BR-0015-0025, BR-0015-0027, BR-0015-0028, BR-0015-0030 to BR-0015-0038, BR-0015-0042, BR-0015-0044, BR-0015-0045, BR-0015-0052, BR-0015-0053, BR-0015-0055, BR-0015-0062, BR-0015-0065, BR-0015-0070 to BR-0015-0073, BR-0015-0075, BR-0015-0077 to BR-0015-0080, BR-0015-0082, BR-0015-0083, BR-0015-0107, BR-0015-0110, BR-0015-0141, BR-0015-0142, BR-0015-0145, BR-0015-0146, BR-0015-0154, BR-0015-0158, BR-0015-0165, BR-0015-0171; BR-0020-0006, BR-0020-0010 to BR-0020-0016, BR-0020-0017, BR-0020-0018, BR-0020-0019; BR-0002-0003; BR-0001-0026; BR-0019-0053, BR-0019-0058; US-0001-0190, US-0001-0191 and US-0001-0209 with their changed criteria and examples; and the criteria and examples of US-0001-0003, US-0001-0004, US-0001-0147, US-0001-0185 to US-0001-0189, US-0001-0194, US-0001-0196 to US-0001-0201, US-0001-0203, US-0001-0204, US-0001-0207 and US-0001-0208 that named a retired route, predicate or stage kind. - Retired: retired BR, retired BR and retired BR, whose plans the route rows now state; the route ids `direct`, `bugfix`, `bounded-change`, `feature` and `discovery`; the stage kind `sdd_delta`; the plan predicates; and the proposal fields `candidateRoute`, `requiredStages` and `optionalSteps`. - Approval: the user approved the routing design, its 39 routes and this change for implementation on 2026-09-28. | SUPERSEDED (by DEC-2049) |
